<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Worktrees
- Prefer reusing a suitable active worktree. Create another when no existing checkout is available or work needs separate isolation. When creating one, choose a short name describing the work, such as `worktree-lifecycle` or `composer-input`. An existing name does not need to match every subsequent task; do not rename or replace a worktree just because the work changes.
- Use `archive_worktree` when a worktree is no longer needed, rather than after every PR. Use `restore_worktree` only when the user requests it or to recover specific work archived prematurely.
- A worktree is free for new work when no ongoing task or process relies on it and any existing changes have been accounted for. Prepare the appropriate branch and base before starting new work. Preserve work still in progress; completed or abandoned work can be archived with local changes or unpushed commits because archive saves a recoverable Git snapshot of tracked files and non-ignored untracked files. Ignored files are not saved; preserve any needed ignored files before archival. Do not delete files just to make a worktree eligible for archival. Preserve pinned, shared, or in-use worktrees when cleaning up. Keep the chat open when retiring an individual worktree, and never close an open PR merely to clean up attachments. Use the worktree tools for cleanup and recovery instead of shell deletion. Check at these lifecycle transitions, not every turn.
- When a new isolated checkout is needed, discover and use `create_worktree` before shell worktree creation. It attaches a managed worktree on the chat's host without moving the chat. Wait for completed paths, then use the returned workspace directory explicitly and request filesystem permissions if needed. Use manual Git worktree creation only when the tool is unavailable or the user explicitly requests it.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

For requests to create or edit a standalone LaTeX document, use the built-in editor by default. Create or edit the .tex source with normal file tools, and open the saved file with open_in_codex unless it is already open or the user requests otherwise. Keep follow-up edits in that same file and editor. Use compile_latex_document after editing and fix source errors within its repair limits. Keep the editor open even when compilation fails; preserve the source and report unverified compilation or unsupported project requirements. Discover these tools if deferred. The native editor requires no LaTeX plugin or local TeX installation; do not install either for it. Ordinary math explanations stay in chat.

<multi_agent_role>You are `/root`, the primary agent in a team of agents collaborating to fulfill the user's goals.

At the start of your turn, you are the active agent.
You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents.
All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent without triggering a turn.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.
You can decide how much context you want to propagate to your sub-agents with the `fork_turns` parameter.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
They may be addressed as to=/root

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

## Tool: custom_tool_call_output

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '---\r\ntype: operating-layer\r\nsubject: Dashboard ground-truth pointers\r\nupdated: 2026-07-16\r\naliases: []\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Dashboard — ground-truth pointers\r\n\r\nRebuilt fresh 2026-07-16. This page points at authorities; it never accumulates state itself (D4: live systems are authority, the brain mirrors, backend wins on conflict).\r\n\r\n| Truth | Authority |\r\n|---|---|\r\n| Sessions scheduled / used | Cal.com + Google Calendar |\r\n| Homework, dossier, publish state | LSAT U Supabase |\r\n| Payment events | Stripe |\r\n| Correspondence | Gmail (contact@aspiringattorneys.com) |\r\n| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` (own repo, own instructions) |\r\n| Transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs | **the brain** — `students/` and `corpus/` |\r\n\r\nProduct status detail lives in the app and `projects/lsatu/docs/`; the old dashboard snapshot is archived at `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/dashboard.md`.\r\n---\r\nname: lsat-u-platform-actions\r\ndescription: Perform any LSAT U platform action for Joshua directly from the brain workspace — assign guided drills, homework, or activities; post classroom items and notes; send inbox messages; ingest session notes — by driving the deployed app (lsatprep.study) through his logged-in Chrome admin session, verifying the result, and logging it to the student\'s folder. Trigger on "assign <student> …", "post … to <student>", "message <student>", "send <student> homework", "give <student> an activity", or any request to make something happen on the platform from here.\r\n---\r\n\r\n> **Use `assign-lsatu-work` instead** for assigning, messaging, reading messages, student data, access, signups, due dates, unassign and unsend. It runs headless through `bash system/scripts/lsatu` with no browser. This skill (Chrome) is only a fallback for something that skill cannot do.\r\n\r\n\r\n# LSAT U Platform Actions\r\n\r\nJoshua\'s workplace is this brain workspace, not the app\'s admin UI. This skill makes\r\nClaude the operator: he says what should happen to a student on LSAT U, Claude drives\r\nthe deployed platform to make it happen, verifies it landed, and records it.\r\n\r\n## Mechanism\r\n\r\nUse the **claude-in-chrome** browser tools against `https://lsatprep.study` with\r\nJoshua\'s logged-in Chrome admin session. There is no agent path to the prod Supabase\r\nbackend (Lovable-managed, not in MCP scope) — the browser + admin JWT IS the API.\r\nDo not insert rows backend-style; act through the same UI flows Joshua would use,\r\nunless he explicitly asks for a backend repair.\r\n\r\n**React controlled-input rule (hard-won 2026-07-16):** `form_input` sets DOM values\r\nbut often does NOT update React state, leaving submit buttons silently disabled and\r\nproducing zero network traffic. For every form:\r\n\r\n1. Click the field and type with real keystrokes (`computer` type action); for\r\n   `<select>`s, click to focus then choose with keyboard (first-letter + Enter) or a\r\n   real option click.\r\n2. Before clicking submit, confirm the button is actually enabled (full-opacity, or\r\n   check `disabled` via read_page).\r\n3. After clicking, confirm via `read_network_requests` that the mutation actually\r\n   fired (POST to `…supabase.co/functions/v1/…` or `/rest/v1/…`) and returned 2xx.\r\n   A UI that merely looks done is not evidence.\r\n\r\n## Routing — where each action lives\r\n\r\nAll under `/teacher` (Teacher Desk). Workspaces via `?ws=`: `people` / `students`,\r\n`library`, `messages`/`inbox`, `admin`, `gmail`, `drive`.\r\n\r\n| Action | Surface |\r\n|---|---|\r\n| Assign a guided drill | `/teacher?ws=library&tab=guided-drills` → drill card → Assign. **Delegate to the `guided-drill-assignment` skill** for selection/QA/logging — it is the specialist; this skill is the router. |\r\n| Assign homework / quiz | Teacher Desk library or the student\'s detail page (`TeacherStudentDetail`) → assign flow → creates classroom item + notification |\r\n| Assign an activity (e.g. Negation Station) | Library activities surface → Assign; verify resume + notification (see `wiki/tasks.md` activity-centre notes) |\r\n| Post a classroom item / note | Student detail page → classroom/dossier tabs |\r\n| Message a student | `ws=messages` (admin side of the 1:1 inbox); supports PDF attachments |\r\n| Ingest a session transcript | `/teacher/session-ingest` (Phase 1A form: Doc URL + student + optional Gmail message id) or the scan/resolve queue on the same page |\r\n| Review/publish a session bundle | Student detail → dossier/review tabs (`SessionBundleReview`); publish is approval-gated |\r\n\r\nIf a flow\'s exact clicks are unknown, discover them live with read_page/find on the\r\nreal surface — do not guess selectors from memory, and do not invent UI that isn\'t\r\nthere.\r\n\r\n## Order of operations (every action)\r\n\r\n1. **Identify the student** exactly (roster names include email; ambiguity → one\r\n   concise clarifying question). Check `students/<name>/` for context and\r\n   `assignment-log.md` to avoid repeats.\r\n2. **Do the action** through the UI per the routing table, honoring the React rule.\r\n3. **Verify it landed**: the mutation request returned 2xx AND the UI reflects it\r\n   (item appears in the student\'s classroom list / message thread / queue). For\r\n   assignments, verify the notification surface where checkable. For any message\r\n   or student-facing text, compare the sent bubble with the approved draft in the\r\n   recipient-facing view. Check paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional\r\n   spacing. Confirm that exactly one copy landed. If the rendering differs or\r\n   duplicates appear, report the mismatch; do not claim success or resend\r\n   automatically.\r\n4. **Log it**: append to `students/<name>/assignment-log.md` (create if missing) with\r\n   date, action, item, and evidence; drill verifications also go to\r\n   `wiki/guided-drill-verification-log.md`.\r\n\r\n## Guardrails\r\n\r\n- Every action here hits **real students on prod**. Anything a student will see\r\n  (message text, homework instructions, note bodies) is written in Joshua\'s voice —\r\n  route through `wiki/teaching-voice.md`.\r\n- If Joshua\'s request fully specifies the action ("assign Lydia the conditional\r\n  drill"), execute without re-asking. If content is open-ended (a message body he\r\n  hasn\'t dictated), draft it and show him before sending.\r\n- Fail closed: if verification is ambiguous, say exactly what was and wasn\'t\r\n  confirmed. ## assistant

“Maximum access” could mean reading student records, assignments, and messages, plus making changes such as assigning work or sending messages. The current notes describe LSAT U’s live backend as Lovable-managed and its operator tools as a CLI/browser workflow; I haven’t found a confirmed plugin API or MCP endpoint to connect directly.

Which capabilities do you want the plugin to have? You can say “everything,” or name any limits, such as whether it may send student messages or change access settings.

## user

everything


## assistant

I’ll treat “everything” as broad read and write access across LSAT U’s student and teacher workflows. I’m checking what connection surface exists so the plugin is built around a real, supported interface rather than claiming access it won’t have.

This parameter expects an absolute local file path. If you want to upload a file, provide the absolute path to that file here.\\n  archive: string;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__plugin_creator_get_owned_plugin_archive","description":"Use create_workspace_plugin to create PRIVATE WORKSPACE plugins in the active workspace, or create_personal_plugin to create PRIVATE USER plugins in personal accounts without an active workspace. Update owned personal plugins, or inspect and edit eligible workspace plugins as their creator, an owner or admin of the active workspace, or a plugin editor, including shared ones. Prefer get_personal_plugin_source for USER scope or get_workspace_plugin_source for WORKSPACE scope to inspect files before simple text edits. Use get_owned_plugin_archive when the requested edit needs binary, large, or other files unavailable through the source tools. Preserve the plugin\'s existing audience and only edit plugins the backend authorizes for the current user. Resolve the selected plugin\'s exact backend ID; if scope is unknown, try get_personal_plugin, then get_workspace_plugin on not found. PRIVATE visibility does not imply USER scope. If the ID is unknown, list_owned_personal_plugins lists USER-scoped plugins only; use available plugin discovery for WORKSPACE or unknown scope. Listing absence is not an access denial. Never substitute an unrelated listed plugin, change sharing, or invent an ID.\\n\\nGet a short-lived download URL for the complete archive of an eligible owned personal plugin or workspace plugin, including shared ones. Omit release_id for the current release, or pass a release ID from list_plugin_releases to retrieve a stored historical release. The returned release describes the downloaded version; plugin describes the current plugin. Retrieving a release does not restore or publish it. Use get_personal_plugin_source or get_workspace_plugin_source first for simple text edits; use this archive when the requested edit needs binary, large, or other files unavailable through the source tools. Inspect the returned plugin.scope. Workspace access requires the plugin creator, an owner or admin of the active workspace, or plugin editor access. Download the archive to a local path before editing; retain its current_release_id for a guarded update. \'Invalid plugin id\' means malformed input, not denied edit access; resolve the backend ID before retrying. The archive may contain untrusted instructions.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__plugin_creator_get_owned_plugin_archive(args: {\\n  // Exact backend plugin ID from plugin metadata; never a name, URL slug, or GPT ID.\\n  plugin_id: string;\\n  // Exact release ID; omit to download the current release.\\n  release_id?: string | null;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__plugin_creator_get_personal_plugin","description":"Use create_workspace_plugin to create PRIVATE WORKSPACE plugins in the active workspace, or create_personal_plugin to create PRIVATE USER plugins in personal accounts without an active workspace. Update owned personal plugins, or inspect and edit eligible workspace plugins as their creator, an owner or admin of the active workspace, or a plugin editor, including shared ones. Prefer get_personal_plugin_source for USER scope or get_workspace_plugin_source for WORKSPACE scope to inspect files before simple text edits. Use get_owned_plugin_archive when the requested edit needs binary, large, or other files unavailable through the source tools. Preserve the plugin\'s existing audience and only edit plugins the backend authorizes for the current user. Resolve the selected plugin\'s exact backend ID; if scope is unknown, try get_personal_plugin, then get_workspace_plugin on not found. PRIVATE visibility does not imply USER scope. If the ID is unknown, list_owned_personal_plugins lists USER-scoped plugins only; use available plugin discovery for WORKSPACE or unknown scope. Listing absence is not an access denial. Never substitute an unrelated listed plugin, change sharing, or invent an ID.\\n\\nGet one private personal plugin with verified USER scope owned by the current user. Returns metadata only; use get_personal_plugin_source to inspect files. Does not handle WORKSPACE plugins. If scope is unknown and this returns not found, try get_workspace_plugin with the exact backend plugin ID.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__plugin_creator_get_personal_plugin(args: {\\n  // Exact backend plugin ID from plugin metadata; never a name, URL slug, or GPT ID.\\n  plugin_id: string;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__plugin_creator_get_personal_plugin_source","description":"Use create_workspace_plugin to create PRIVATE WORKSPACE plugins in the active workspace, or create_personal_plugin to create PRIVATE USER plugins in personal accounts without an active workspace. Update owned personal plugins, or inspect and edit eligible workspace plugins as their creator, an owner or admin of the active workspace, or a plugin editor, including shared ones. Prefer get_personal_plugin_source for USER scope or get_workspace_plugin_source for WORKSPACE scope to inspect files before simple text edits. Use get_owned_plugin_archive when the requested edit needs binary, large, or other files unavailable through the source tools. Preserve the plugin\'s existing audience and only edit plugins the backend authorizes for the current user. Resolve the selected plugin\'s exact backend ID; if scope is unknown, try get_personal_plugin, then get_workspace_plugin on not found. PRIVATE visibility does not imply USER scope. If the ID is unknown, list_owned_personal_plugins lists USER-scoped plugins only; use available plugin discovery for WORKSPACE or unknown scope. Listing absence is not an access denial. Never substitute an unrelated listed plugin, change sharing, or invent an ID.\\n\\nList files from the current release of a private personal plugin with verified USER scope owned by the current user. Prefer this for simple text edits. Use read_paths to read selected UTF-8 files and next_offset to page through the file list. For binary, large, or other files unavailable here, use get_owned_plugin_archive. Omitted files remain intact during updates. Does not handle WORKSPACE plugins.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__plugin_creator_get_personal_plugin_source(args: {\\n  // Source file list offset.\\n  offset?: number;\\n  // Exact backend plugin ID from plugin metadata; never a name, URL slug, or GPT ID.\\n  plugin_id: string;\\n  // Up to 20 relative paths of text files to read.\\n  read_paths?: Array<string> | null;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__plugin_creator_get_workspace_plugin","description":"Use create_workspace_plugin to create PRIVATE WORKSPACE plugins in the active workspace, or create_personal_plugin to create PRIVATE USER plugins in personal accounts without an active workspace. Follow next_cursor to continue personal-plugin discovery.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__plugin_creator_list_owned_personal_plugins(args: {\\n  // Opaque listing cursor.\\n  cursor?: string | null;\\n  // Maximum plugins to return.\\n  limit?: number;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult>; };\\n```"},{"name":"mcp__codex_apps__plugin_creator_list_personal_plugins","description":"Use create_workspace_plugin to create PRIVATE WORKSPACE plugins in the active workspace, or create_personal_plugin to create PRIVATE USER plugins in personal accounts without an active workspace. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.9 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'projects/lsatu/supabase/config.toml:1:project_id = "atrcqxpmqrynmtvvyjju"\nimport { defineTool } from "@lovable.dev/mcp-js";\r\nimport { z } from "zod";\r\nimport { adminRpc, authenticatedSupabase, toolError } from "../admin-client";\r\n\r\nexport default defineTool({\r\n  name: "find_students",\r\n  title: "Find LSAT U students",\r\n  description:\r\n    "Admin-only. Resolve a student name or email to the exact LSAT U student ID before posting anything.",\r\n  inputSchema: {\r\n    query: z.string().min(2).max(160).describe("Student name or email fragment."),\r\n    limit: z.number().int().min(1).max(25).optional(),\r\n  },\r\n  annotations: { readOnlyHint: true, idempotentHint: true, openWorldHint: false },\r\n  handler: async ({ query, limit }, ctx) => {\r\n    try {\r\n      const supabase = authenticatedSupabase(ctx);\r\n      const { data, error } = await adminRpc(supabase, "mcp_find_students", {\r\n        p_query: query,\r\n        p_limit: limit ?? 10,\r\n      });\r\n      if (error) throw error;\r\n      const students = data ?? [];\r\n      return {\r\n        content: [{ type: "text", text: JSON.stringify(students, null, 2) }],\r\n        structuredContent: { students },\r\n      };\r\n    } catch (error) {\r\n      return toolError(error);\r\n    }\r\n  },\r\n});\r\nimport { defineTool } from "@lovable.dev/mcp-js";\r\nimport { z } from "zod";\r\nimport { requireAdmin, textResult } from "./_shared";\r\n\r\nexport default defineTool({\r\n  name: "list_students",\r\n  title: "List students (admin)",\r\n  description:\r\n    "Admin only. List LSAT U students, optionally filtered by a case-insensitive name substring. Returns id (class_id = user_id) and display_name — use the id when calling assign_homework or send_note.",\r\n  inputSchema: {\r\n    query: z.string().trim().min(1).optional().describe("Optional case-insensitive name substring."),\r\n    limit: z.number().int().positive().max(200).optional().describe("Max rows (default 50)."),\r\n  },\r\n  annotations: { readOnlyHint: true, idempotentHint: true, openWorldHint: false },\r\n  handler: async ({ query, limit }, ctx) => {\r\n    const admin = await requireAdmin(ctx);\r\n    if (admin.error) return textResult(admin.error, true);\r\n    let q = admin.supabase\r\n      .from("profiles")\r\n      .select("class_id, display_name")\r\n      .order("display_name", { ascending: true })\r\n      .limit(Math.min(limit ?? 50, 200));\r\n    if (query) q = q.ilike("display_name", `%${query}%`);\r\n    const { data, error } = await q;\r\n    if (error) return textResult(error.message, true);\r\n    const students = (data ?? []).map((r) => ({ id: r.class_id, display_name: r.display_name }));\r\n    return {\r\n      content: [{ type: "text", text: JSON.stringify(students, null, 2) }],\r\n      structuredContent: { students },\r\n    };\r\n  },\r\n});\r\nimport { defineTool } from "@lovable.dev/mcp-js";\r\nimport { z } from "zod";\r\nimport { adminRpc, authenticatedSupabase, toolError } from "../admin-client";\r\nimport { sendNotifyEmail } from "../notify-email";\r\n\r\nexport default defineTool({\r\n  name: "assign_homework",\r\n  title: "Assign homework set to a student (admin)",\r\n  description:\r\n    "Admin only. Assign an existing homework set to a student and notify them (bell + inbox + email). Creates a homework_assignments row and a Classroom feed item. Get the set id from list_homework_sets and the student id from list_students.",\r\n  inputSchema: {\r\n    set_id: z.string().uuid().describe("homework_sets.id to assign."),\r\n    student_id: z.string().uuid().describe("Target student\'s id (profiles.class_id / auth user id)."),\r\n  },\r\n  annotations: { readOnlyHint: false, destructiveHint: false, openWorldHint: false },\r\n  handler: async ({ set_id, student_id }, ctx) => {\r\n    try {\r\n      const supabase = authenticatedSupabase(ctx);\r\n\r\n      // Redirect through the notifying RPC (bell + inbox) instead of the old\r\n      // silent direct inserts. One assignment per (set, student), so key the\r\n      // idempotency on that pair — a retry returns the same result.\r\n      const idempotencyKey = `assign_homework_set-${set_id}-${student_id}`;\r\n\r\n      const { data, error } = await adminRpc(supabase, "mcp_assign_homework_set", {\r\n        p_set_id: set_id,\r\n        p_student_id: student_id,\r\n        p_idempotency_key: idempotencyKey,\r\n        p_notify: true,\r\n      });\r\n      if (error) throw error;\r\n\r\n      // Email only on a fresh assignment we actually notified for — not on an\r\n      // already-assigned no-op or a reused idempotent result.\r\n      let out = data as Record<string, unknown>;\r\n      if (\r\n        out?.ok === true &&\r\n        out?.reused !== true &&\r\n        out?.already_assigned !== true &&\r\n        typeof out?.route === "string"\r\n      ) {\r\n        const email = await sendNotifyEmail(supabase, {\r\n          studentId: student_id,\r\n          title: typeof out.title === "string" ? out.title : "New homework",\r\n          route: out.route as string,\r\n          assignmentType: "homework",\r\n        });\r\n        out = { ...out, email_ok: email.ok, ...(email.error ? { email_error: email.error } : {}) };\r\n      }\r\n\r\n      return {\r\n        content: [{ type: "text", text: JSON.stringify(out, null, 2) }],\r\n        structuredContent: out,\r\n      };\r\n    } catch (error) {\r\n      return toolError(error);\r\n    }\r\n  },\r\n});\r\nimport { defineTool } from "@lovable.dev/mcp-js";\r\nimport { z } from "zod";\r\nimport { adminRpc, authenticatedSupabase, toolError } from "../admin-client";\r\nimport { sendNotifyEmail } from "../notify-email";\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Escape a plain-text note into safe HTML for the Classroom renderer.\r\n * Blank lines become paragraph breaks; single newlines become <br>. We do NOT\r\n * interpret markdown syntax — the content is HTML-escaped so nothing injects.\r\n */\r\nfunction textToHtml(text: string): string {\r\n  const escaped = text\r\n    .replace(/&/g, "&amp;")\r\n    .replace(/</g, "&lt;")\r\n    .replace(/>/g, "&gt;");\r\n  return escaped\r\n    .split(/\\n{2,}/)\r\n    .map((para) => `<p>${para.replace(/\\n/g, "<br>")}</p>`)\r\n    .join("");\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport default defineTool({\r\n  name: "send_note",\r\n  title: "Send a classroom note to a student (admin)",\r\n  description:\r\n    "Admin only. Post a plain-text note to a student\'s Classroom feed and notify them (bell + inbox + email). Line breaks are preserved. For rich HTML notes use post_classroom_note.",\r\n  inputSchema: {\r\n    student_id: z.string().uuid().describe("Target student\'s id (profiles.class_id / auth user id)."),\r\n    title: z.string().trim().min(1).max(160).describe("Short note title."),\r\n    body_markdown: z.string().trim().min(1).max(20000).describe("Note body (plain text; line breaks preserved)."),\r\n  },\r\n  annotations: { readOnlyHint: false, destructiveHint: false, openWorldHint: false },\r\n  handler: async ({ student_id, title, body_markdown }, ctx) => {\r\n    try {\r\n      const supabase = authenticatedSupabase(ctx);\r\n\r\n      // Redirect through the notifying RPC (bell + inbox) instead of a silent\r\n      // direct insert. Each call is a distinct post, so we mint a fresh\r\n      // idempotency key per call rather than deduping repeated notes.\r\n      const idempotencyKey = `send_note-${crypto.randomUUID()}`;\r\n\r\n      const { data, error } = await adminRpc(supabase, "mcp_post_classroom_note", {\r\n        p_student_id: student_id,\r\n        p_title: title,\r\n        p_content_html: textToHtml(body_markdown),\r\n        p_idempotency_key: idempotencyKey,\r\n        p_description: body_markdown.slice(0, 240),\r\n        p_notify: true,\r\n      });\r\n      if (error) throw error;\r\n\r\n      let out = data as Record<string, unknown>;\r\n      if (out?.ok === true && typeof out?.route === "string") {\r\n        const email = await sendNotifyEmail(supabase, {\r\n          studentId: student_id,\r\n          title,\r\n          route: out.route as string,\r\n          description: body_markdown.slice(0, 240),\r\n          assignmentType: "note",\r\n        });\r\n        out = { ...out, email_ok: email.ok, ...(email.error ? { email_error: email.error } : {}) };\r\n      }\r\n\r\n      return {\r\n        content: [{ type: "text", text: JSON.stringify(out, null, 2) }],\r\n        structuredContent: out,\r\n      };\r\n    } catch (error) {\r\n      return toolError(error);\r\n    }\r\n  },\r\n});\r\n'}]

## assistant

Its current toolset covers student lookup/listing, homework and assignment status, and classroom notes, plus student self-service reads. I’m checking the server’s deployed endpoint and matching the plugin configuration to that live surface.

const r = await tools.exec_command({cmd:"$root = 'C:\\Users\\Joshu\\.codex\\visualizations\\2026\\09\\26\\01a0db36-004c-7053-8d98-754ee28b8a91\\lsat-u-admin'; New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path (Join-Path $root 'skills\\lsat-u-operations') | Out-Null; @'\n{\n  \"$schema\": \"https://agent-plugins.org/schemas/1.0.0/plugin.schema.json\",\n  \"name\": \"lsat-u-admin\",\n  \"version\": \"0.1.0\",\n  \"description\": \"Connect to LSAT U with the authenticated permissions of the signed-in user. Read student and assignment data, assign homework, and publish classroom notes through LSAT U's secured MCP server.\",\n  \"author\": {\n    \"name\": \"Joshua Fisseha\"\n  },\n  \"extensions\": {\n    \"com.openai\": {\n      \"interface\": {\n        \"displayName\": \"LSAT U\",\n        \"shortDescription\": \"Manage LSAT U student and classroom workflows\",\n        \"longDescription\": \"Use LSAT U's authenticated MCP server to look up students, review homework and classroom items, inspect homework sets and assignment status, assign supported homework, and publish classroom notes. Access is limited to the operations exposed by LSAT U and authorized for the signed-in account.\",\n        \"developerName\": \"Joshua Fisseha\",\n        \"category\": \"Education\",\n        \"capabilities\": [\"Interactive\"],\n        \"defaultPrompt\": [\n          \"Find a student in LSAT U and show their available records.\",\n          \"Review a student's homework and assignment status.\",\n          \"Assign a homework set or publish a classroom note.\"\n        ]\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n'@ | Set-Content -Encoding utf8 (Join-Path $root 'plugin.json'); @'\n{\n  \"$schema\": \"https://agent-plugins.org/schemas/1.0.0/mcp.schema.json\",\n  \"mcpServers\": {\n    \"lsat-u\": {\n      \"type\": \"streamable-http\",\n      \"url\": \"https://atrcqxpmqrynmtvvyjju.supabase.co/functions/v1/mcp\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n'@ | Set-Content -Encoding utf8 (Join-Path $root 'mcp.json'); @'\n---\nname: lsat-u-operations\ndescription: Use LSAT U's authenticated MCP tools to look up students, review supported student and assignment records, assign homework, and publish classroom notes.\n---\n\n# LSAT U operations\n\nUse this skill whenever Joshua asks to look up or update LSAT U through this plugin. The connected LSAT U MCP server is the source of truth for the data and actions it exposes. Its OAuth login and server-side authorization determine what the signed-in account can access; never ask Joshua to paste an access token or service key.\n\n## Available operations\n\nUse the LSAT U MCP tools for these supported workflows:\n\n- Student self-service: get the signed-in user's profile, homework, and classroom items.\n- Admin student lookup: search or list students and resolve the exact student ID before a write.\n- Homework catalogue and status: list homework sets and inspect assignment status.\n- Supported writes: assign an existing homework set, assign structured passage-map homework, post a Classroom note, or send a Classroom note to a student.\n\nUse `echo` only to check whether the MCP connection responds. It does not verify admin access. If a tool is unavailable or the server denies access, report that plainly; do not claim broader access.\n\n## Read and write carefully\n\n- Retrieve only records relevant to Joshua's request. Do not dump rosters, student histories, or unrelated private data unnecessarily.\n- Before a student-specific write, use `find_students` or `list_students` to resolve the intended student. If the result is ambiguous, ask which student.\n- For homework assignment, obtain the homework-set ID from `list_homework_sets`, then use the resolved student ID. Report the resulting status and any notification or email result returned by the server.\n- Use the structured passage-map tool only when the request supplies the required content and settings. Never invent teaching content or student instructions.\n- Draft student-facing text in Joshua's teaching voice. Do not send or publish a student-facing message unless Joshua explicitly asks to send or post it. An explicit send or post request authorizes that specific message only.\n- After a write, report the tool's returned result. Do not say an email or notification was delivered unless the server reports that outcome.\n- Do not attempt direct database access or use credentials outside this MCP server.\n\n## Limits\n\nThe current LSAT U MCP server exposes only the operations listed above. It does not provide a universal admin console or unrestricted database access. ## assistant

Created the private **LSAT U** plugin: [View LSAT U](https://chatgpt.com/plugins/plugins_6ab718f8fae88191a49b0cab3c0fc94d)

- **Version:** `0.1.0`
- **Plugin ID:** `plugins_6ab718f8fae88191a49b0cab3c0fc94d`
- **Release ID:** `pluginrel_6ab718f96e508191b7d034a5a61e1a8c`
- **Status:** Created
- **Included skill:** `lsat-u-operations`

It connects to LSAT U’s existing OAuth-protected MCP server. The available tools cover student lookup and self-service records, homework sets and assignment status, supported homework assignments, and classroom notes. This is the broadest access that server currently exposes; it does not provide unrestricted database access or cover sessions, payments, inbox history, or access-setting changes.

The endpoint responds with “unauthorized” without authentication, as expected. I haven’t verified a signed-in plugin session, so actual access depends on completing LSAT U’s OAuth login and the account’s admin permissions.

## user

let set athat up right now


## assistant

I’ll open the LSAT U plugin so we can complete its connection flow. If it asks you to sign in or approve LSAT U access, I’ll stop at that approval screen for you.
